# trust-json corpus manifest — the acceptance gate's SAMPLE, pinned.
#
# One checked-in JSON document per line, path relative to the repository root,
# sorted. `tests/acceptance_gate.rs` parses every one of these with BOTH this
# crate and the incumbent `serde_json`, and requires the two to agree on the
# parse, on the tree, and on the exact BYTES of both the compact and the pretty
# re-serialization.
#
# REGENERATE (and read the diff before committing it):
#
#   git ls-files -z -- '*.json' \
#     | tr '\0' '\n' \
#     | grep -v '^first-party/' | grep -v '^vendor/' \
#     | while read -r f; do [ "$(stat -f %z "$f")" -lt 1048576 ] && echo "$f"; done \
#     | sort
#
# CHECKED AT LANDING (2026-11-10, docs/DECISION_QUEUE.md
# CORPUS-MEMBERSHIP-AT-LANDING-1). The push row `fast.corpus-membership`
# (scripts/check_corpus_membership.py) applies this rule to the pushed tree,
# so a landing that adds or removes a document must carry its re-pin.
# `python3 scripts/check_corpus_membership.py --write` regenerates this body
# from the checkout's index under exactly the command above, keeping this
# header; the row refuses a header whose command it does not apply.
#
# 2026-11-10: include the three checked-in lean-native-linker qualification
# reports missed by the earlier landing: 7142 -> 7145 documents, no removals.
# 2026-11-10: include three dependency filename inventory reports: 7146 -> 7149.
#
# SCOPE, and why it stops where it does.
#
#   * `git ls-files`, not `find`: checked-in documents only. Everything under
#     `build/`, `target/` and the various `*.gitignore`d dumps is machine- and
#     moment-specific, and a corpus that changes when you build is not a pin.
#   * `first-party/` is EXCLUDED. Those are submodules; their content changes
#     whenever a gitlink moves, which would turn every pin bump into a red gate
#     for reasons that have nothing to do with a JSON reader.
#   * `vendor/` is EXCLUDED for the same reason.
#   * Documents of 1 MiB or more are EXCLUDED. Measured 2026-08-23: the tracked
#     `*.json` set is 3217 files / 1135.4 MiB, and 263 of those files carry
#     1023.2 MiB of it — 90% of the bytes in 8% of the files. Every one of the
#     263 is a machine-generated dump (`reports/trust-ir-producer-baseline-*`
#     675.5 MiB / 178 files, `crates/trust-clean/fixtures/*` 352.2 MiB / 2338
#     files of which the large tail, `src/llvm-project` 27.9 MiB / 161 files),
#     i.e. the same handful of row shapes repeated a million times. They add
#     minutes of wall clock and no grammar the remaining 2954 files do not
#     already carry — which is not an assertion, it is checked:
#     `corpus_still_exercises_every_measured_construct` puts a floor under each
#     construct this crate can get wrong, and that test reads THIS list.
#
# NO THIRD PREFIX EXCLUSION. Asked and MEASURED 2026-09-18, because the obvious
# response to the churn below is "stop sampling `reports/`".
#
#   The sample grew 3325 documents in four days (2791 of them under `reports/`,
#   which gained 1881 files on 2026-09-17 and 915 on 2026-09-18 — dated,
#   append-only session evidence written in bulk by other lanes), and that made
#   this gate red three times in four days. Excluding `reports/` would have
#   ended the churn. It would also have broken the gate's own construct floors:
#
#     construct          full sample   without `reports/`   floor
#     non-ASCII key            4              1               2   <- BELOW
#     empty object            96             47              50   <- BELOW
#     control char in string 125             23              20
#     quote/backslash        178             54              50
#
#   Three of the FOUR documents in this entire repository that carry a
#   non-ASCII object key live under `reports/` (the July
#   `trust-ir-producer-baseline-2026-07-01/postbuild-wave{U,V}-burnin*/data.json`
#   set); the fourth is `scripts/trust_ir_tag_families.json`. The bulk
#   machine-generated directory is also the rare-construct carrier, so the
#   cheap scope narrowing is a real loss of coverage, not a tidy-up. The 1 MiB
#   cap above is a different argument and still holds: it drops bytes, not
#   constructs, and the floors were measured across it.
#
#   `the_membership_rule_is_exactly_the_documented_rule` pins this: it re-derives
#   the rule from `git ls-files` independently of `recompute_membership`, so a
#   fourth exclusion added to the code fails there and not only as a floor that
#   someone could lower in the same patch.
#
# WHY THIS FILE HOLDS PATHS AND NOT PER-FILE DIGESTS.
#
# The same reasoning `crates/trust-toml/tests/corpus_manifest.txt` records, and
# it applies with more force here: these 6920 documents include compiler test
# fixtures, coverage dumps and scorecards that are rewritten by ordinary work in
# this tree, so a digest list would be red most mornings and the only available
# response would be mechanical regeneration. A gate whose refresh is a reflex is
# not evidence.
#
# What the sample-shift lesson is actually about is the SAMPLE — files
# appearing and, far worse, quietly DISAPPEARING, so that a shrinking corpus
# reads as a green run. That property is delivered in full by pinning the path
# list: `corpus_membership_matches_the_pinned_manifest` recomputes the list from
# `git ls-files` under exactly the rule above and fails on any difference in
# either direction, printing the added and removed paths.
#

# WHY THE LIST IS STILL PINNED, NOT DERIVED (asked and decided 2026-10-08,
# JSON-CORPUS-PIN-1). Every re-pin in this log since 2026-10-01 was additions only,
# found red on main by someone else: 40 (14ea013bfe), 13 (f99836cc18), 27
# (c9e3451867), 563 (here). Deriving the corpus from `git ls-files` at test time
# would end that, and would differentially test a new document the moment it lands.
# It would also end the deletion guard: a document deleted from the tree would leave
# a derived corpus smaller and still green, which is the shrinking-corpus hazard this
# file exists for. A one-sided pin (pinned paths must exist, additions are free) keeps
# a deletion of a PINNED document red, but leaves every document added since the last
# pin unguarded against a later deletion. Both are weaker, so neither was made. The
# churn is a scoping defect of the push lane, fixed there: until 2026-10-08
# scripts/check_crates_tests_scoped.py ran this binary only when trust-json itself
# changed; it now runs it for any push that adds, deletes or edits a `*.json` outside
# `first-party/` and `vendor/` (CORPUS_INSTRUMENTS), so the commit that adds a document
# meets this gate before main does.

#

#       merges it rides on (linux lane + gate-RCA fix-forwards) changed the
#       checked-in JSON population. Read the path diff in the commit; the
#       acceptance test re-parses every listed document under both readers.

#       the complete parser differential remains required after this refresh.
